People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not taking up time someone else might need more, but... well, I guess I'm here because I've been having a hard time at work. I'm a music director, and... gosh, I feel a little silly even saying it out loud. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining. 

But for almost two years now, ever since this one big commission, I’ve just been unable to shake the certainty that a few people in the ensemble are... well, that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. It's just little things, you know? Like sudden schedule changes, or weird seating choices, or just these little comments they make during rehearsals. And I know it probably sounds strange, because they'll show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why things were changed, but... I just know what's really going on, even if I can't get anyone else to see it. 

I just can't shake it, and it's been really distracting. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this okay.

Yes, um... I really do. I know it probably sounds like I'm just being difficult or, I don't know, paranoid, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm usually very easy to work with, I promise. But... yes.

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I... I mean, I try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just assumes the worst of people. But... gosh, it's just been going on for almost two years now, ever since that commission. 

I know it sounds like maybe I'm just reading too much into things, and I've tried to tell myself that. It's just little things, you know? Like sudden schedule changes, or weird seating choices, or just these little comments they make during rehearsals. And I know it probably sounds strange, because they'll show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why things were changed, but... I just know what's really going on, even if I can't get anyone else to see it. \n\nI just can't shake it, and it's been really distracting. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are undermining your work in secret, even when there's evidence to explain the changes they've made?\n\nPatient: Yes, um... I really do. I know it probably sounds like I'm just being difficult or, I don't know, paranoid, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm usually very easy to work with, I promise. But... yes. \n\nEven when they show me an email saying, you know, \"Oh, the venue changed the rehearsal time,\" or \"We had to move the violins because of the acoustics,\" I just... I know that's not the real reason. It's been going on for almost two years now, ever since that big commission, and it's always those same few people. It's the schedule changes, the seating, the little comments... it all just adds up to them trying to quietly undermine me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds stubborn when they have these supposed explanations, but I just can't shake the certainty that they're doing it on purpose. Does that... I mean, am I making any sense? I hope I'm not sounding too unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are actively working against you outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all. I really don't think they're doing anything outside of work. I hope I didn't make it sound like I think they're, you know, following me around or anything crazy like that. \n\nHonestly, outside of this one specific issue with those few ensemble members, everything else is going really well. I mean, I'm still meeting all my deadlines, and my composing is going great. I even make sure to run most mornings to stay healthy, and... well, things at home with my wife are completely steady. We're doing fine. \n\nSo it's really just this one isolated thing at work. I'm still functioning perfectly normally everywhere else, I promise. Is that... is that helpful to know? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting these actions by your ensemble members?\n\nPatient: I... I mean, I try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just assumes the worst of people. But... gosh, it's just been going on for almost two years now, ever since that commission. \n\nI know it sounds like maybe I'm just reading too much into things, and I've tried to tell myself that. But it's always those same few people, and it's always these specific things—the schedule changes, the seating arrangements, the little comments during rehearsal. And even when they pull up an email or a note that's supposed to explain why a change was made... I just, I can't shake the absolute certainty that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably isn't what you want to hear, and I really hope I don't sound completely unreasonable. I just... I can't see it any other way, even though I try. Does that make me sound too rigid? I really am trying to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that unrelated things, such as news stories, conversations of others, or even songs, are somehow related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, definitely not. I mean, I know sometimes people can read into things like that, but I've never experienced anything like that. \n\nWhen I watch the news or listen to music, it's just... you know, just the news or just a song. I don't think it has anything to do with me or the situation at work. I really try to keep things in perspective, and I promise I'm not looking for signs or anything out of the ordinary. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that I'm keeping a very level head about everything else. I'm not letting this work thing spill over into how I see the rest of the world. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted your belief that these ensemble members are undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes, because I know that's probably what a reasonable person would do. And I really, really try to be reasonable. I don't want to be difficult or cause problems for anyone. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I need to be honest with you... no, I haven't really doubted it. Like I said, it's just this absolute certainty I have. It's been almost two years now, right after that commission, and it's always those same few people. It's the schedule changes, the seating choices, the little comments during rehearsal... it just feels so clear to me that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nEven when they show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why they did something... I just can't shake the feeling. I know that probably sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry if it makes me seem stubborn. I'm really not trying to be. I just... I can't help but see it that way. Is it... is it bad that I feel so certain about it? I hope you're not disappointed in me for saying that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your certainty about being undermined is an incorrect interpretation of the situation?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's what I'm supposed to say, right? That I should be open to being wrong. And I try so hard to be the kind of person who gives everyone the benefit of the doubt. I don't want you to think I'm just this paranoid, difficult person who can't take criticism or work well with others. \n\nBut... gosh, I'm sorry, I just... I can't. I'm here because for almost two years now, ever since that commission, I just haven't been able to shake this absolute certainty. It's just those few people in the ensemble, and it's the schedule changes, the seating choices, the little comments during rehearsals... they're just quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nEven when they pull up those emails or notes that supposedly explain why they did something... it doesn't change how certain I feel about what's really happening. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm frustrating you. I just... I can't see it as an incorrect interpretation, as much as I wish I could. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not being too difficult by saying that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I'm a music director, and... gosh, I feel a little silly even saying it out loud. I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining. \n\nBut for almost two years now, ever since this one big commission, I’ve just been unable to shake the certainty that a few people in the ensemble are... well, that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. It's just little things, you know? Like sudden schedule changes, or weird seating choices, or just these little comments they make during rehearsals. And I know it probably sounds strange, because they'll show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why things were changed, but... I just know what's really going on, even if I can't get anyone else to see it. \n\nI just can't shake it, and it's been really distracting. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are undermining your work in secret, even when there's evidence to explain the changes they've made?\n\nPatient: Yes, um... I really do. I know it probably sounds like I'm just being difficult or, I don't know, paranoid, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm usually very easy to work with, I promise. But... yes. \n\nEven when they show me an email saying, you know, \"Oh, the venue changed the rehearsal time,\" or \"We had to move the violins because of the acoustics,\" I just... I know that's not the real reason. It's been going on for almost two years now, ever since that big commission, and it's always those same few people. It's the schedule changes, the seating, the little comments... it all just adds up to them trying to quietly undermine me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds stubborn when they have these supposed explanations, but I just can't shake the certainty that they're doing it on purpose. Does that... I mean, am I making any sense? I hope I'm not sounding too unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are actively working against you outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all. I really don't think they're doing anything outside of work. I hope I didn't make it sound like I think they're, you know, following me around or anything crazy like that. \n\nHonestly, outside of this one specific issue with those few ensemble members, everything else is going really well. I mean, I'm still meeting all my deadlines, and my composing is going great. I even make sure to run most mornings to stay healthy, and... well, things at home with my wife are completely steady. We're doing fine. \n\nSo it's really just this one isolated thing at work. I'm still functioning perfectly normally everywhere else, I promise. Is that... is that helpful to know? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting these actions by your ensemble members?\n\nPatient: I... I mean, I try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just assumes the worst of people. But... gosh, it's just been going on for almost two years now, ever since that commission. \n\nI know it sounds like maybe I'm just reading too much into things, and I've tried to tell myself that. But it's always those same few people, and it's always these specific things—the schedule changes, the seating arrangements, the little comments during rehearsal. And even when they pull up an email or a note that's supposed to explain why a change was made... I just, I can't shake the absolute certainty that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably isn't what you want to hear, and I really hope I don't sound completely unreasonable. I just... I can't see it any other way, even though I try. Does that make me sound too rigid? I really am trying to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that unrelated things, such as news stories, conversations of others, or even songs, are somehow related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, definitely not. I mean, I know sometimes people can read into things like that, but I've never experienced anything like that. \n\nWhen I watch the news or listen to music, it's just... you know, just the news or just a song. I don't think it has anything to do with me or the situation at work. I really try to keep things in perspective, and I promise I'm not looking for signs or anything out of the ordinary. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that I'm keeping a very level head about everything else. I'm not letting this work thing spill over into how I see the rest of the world. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted your belief that these ensemble members are undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes, because I know that's probably what a reasonable person would do. And I really, really try to be reasonable. I don't want to be difficult or cause problems for anyone. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I need to be honest with you... no, I haven't really doubted it. Like I said, it's just this absolute certainty I have. It's been almost two years now, right after that commission, and it's always those same few people. It's the schedule changes, the seating choices, the little comments during rehearsal... it just feels so clear to me that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nEven when they show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why they did something... I just can't shake the feeling. I know that probably sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry if it makes me seem stubborn. I'm really not trying to be. I just... I can't help but see it that way. Is it... is it bad that I feel so certain about it? I hope you're not disappointed in me for saying that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your certainty about being undermined is an incorrect interpretation of the situation?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's what I'm supposed to say, right? That I should be open to being wrong. And I try so hard to be the kind of person who gives everyone the benefit of the doubt. I don't want you to think I'm just this paranoid, difficult person who can't take criticism or work well with others. \n\nBut... gosh, I'm sorry, I just... I can't. I'm here because for almost two years now, ever since that commission, I just haven't been able to shake this absolute certainty. It's just those few people in the ensemble, and it's the schedule changes, the seating choices, the little comments during rehearsals... they're just quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nEven when they pull up those emails or notes that supposedly explain why they did something... it doesn't change how certain I feel about what's really happening. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm frustrating you. I just... I can't see it as an incorrect interpretation, as much as I wish I could. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not being too difficult by saying that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people outside of your ensemble are watching you or talking about you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I mean, I know how that sounds, and I would tell you if I did, I promise. But no, it's really just... just those few people at work. \n\nWhen I'm out at the grocery store or, you know, just walking around, I don't feel like anyone is paying attention to me at all. I'm just a normal person going about my day. I don't think strangers care about me or what I'm doing. \n\nI really try to keep this whole thing contained to just that specific situation with the ensemble. I'm not... I'm not paranoid about the whole world, you know? I hope that makes you feel a little better about my situation. I'm really trying to be as grounded as possible about everything else. Is that... is that a good sign, that it's just the one thing?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I... I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record today, and I really apologize for being so stuck on this. I'd love to sit here and tell you that, yes, maybe in a few months it'll all just blow over and I'll see it differently. That's what a good, professional collaborator would do, right?

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, yes, absolutely. I really want to be clear about that. Outside of work, I'm a very trusting person. I have good friends, I get along with my neighbors... I don't go around second-guessing everyone I meet. 

I know it might seem like I'm just a suspicious person because of how I feel about those few people in the ensemble, but I promise that's not who I am generally. I trust my family, my friends... even just people I interact with day-to-day, like at the coffee shop or whatever. I don't think everyone is out to get me. 

I really try to be a positive, open person in my regular life. I hope that helps show that I'm trying to keep a good perspective on things overall. Does that... does that sound like a healthy way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm not letting this work thing ruin my whole outlook on life.

I hope you can understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally trust other people outside of this situation with the ensemble?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. I... I don't know. That's a really good question. I try to be open-minded, I really do. But... I'm here because for almost two years now, I've just been unable to shake this... this absolute certainty about what's happening. 

Since the commission, it's just been this pattern with a few people in the ensemble. The sudden schedule changes, the weird seating choices, the little comments they make during rehearsals... it all feels so deliberate. And they'll show me emails or rehearsal notes that supposedly explain why they did this or that, like, "Oh, it was just a logistical thing," or "We had a conflict." But... it doesn't change what I know is happening. The emails just feel like cover-ups, you know? 

I really wish I could just look at a schedule change and say, "Okay, that's fine," but my brain just won't let it go. It feels like they're quietly undermining my work, and no piece of paper they show me seems to fix that feeling. 

Does that make sense? I hope I don't sound completely stubborn. I really want to be flexible and easy to work with... I just can't seem to turn off this feeling that they're working against me. Is it okay that I feel that way, even with the "proof" they try to show me?

It's just little things, you know? Like sudden schedule changes, or weird seating choices, or just these little comments they make during rehearsals. And I know it probably sounds strange, because they'll show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why things were changed, but... I just know what's really going on, even if I can't get anyone else to see it. \n\nI just can't shake it, and it's been really distracting. Is that... does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining this okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are undermining your work in secret, even when there's evidence to explain the changes they've made?\n\nPatient: Yes, um... I really do. I know it probably sounds like I'm just being difficult or, I don't know, paranoid, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm usually very easy to work with, I promise. But... yes. \n\nEven when they show me an email saying, you know, \"Oh, the venue changed the rehearsal time,\" or \"We had to move the violins because of the acoustics,\" I just... I know that's not the real reason. It's been going on for almost two years now, ever since that big commission, and it's always those same few people. It's the schedule changes, the seating, the little comments... it all just adds up to them trying to quietly undermine me. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds stubborn when they have these supposed explanations, but I just can't shake the certainty that they're doing it on purpose. Does that... I mean, am I making any sense? I hope I'm not sounding too unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are actively working against you outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no, not at all. I really don't think they're doing anything outside of work. I hope I didn't make it sound like I think they're, you know, following me around or anything crazy like that. \n\nHonestly, outside of this one specific issue with those few ensemble members, everything else is going really well. I mean, I'm still meeting all my deadlines, and my composing is going great. I even make sure to run most mornings to stay healthy, and... well, things at home with my wife are completely steady. We're doing fine. \n\nSo it's really just this one isolated thing at work. I'm still functioning perfectly normally everywhere else, I promise. Is that... is that helpful to know? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that you might be misinterpreting these actions by your ensemble members?\n\nPatient: I... I mean, I try to be open-minded, I really do. I don't want to be the kind of person who just assumes the worst of people. But... gosh, it's just been going on for almost two years now, ever since that commission. \n\nI know it sounds like maybe I'm just reading too much into things, and I've tried to tell myself that. But it's always those same few people, and it's always these specific things—the schedule changes, the seating arrangements, the little comments during rehearsal. And even when they pull up an email or a note that's supposed to explain why a change was made... I just, I can't shake the absolute certainty that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that probably isn't what you want to hear, and I really hope I don't sound completely unreasonable. I just... I can't see it any other way, even though I try. Does that make me sound too rigid? I really am trying to be cooperative, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that unrelated things, such as news stories, conversations of others, or even songs, are somehow related to you or your situation?\n\nPatient: Oh, no. No, definitely not. I mean, I know sometimes people can read into things like that, but I've never experienced anything like that. \n\nWhen I watch the news or listen to music, it's just... you know, just the news or just a song. I don't think it has anything to do with me or the situation at work. I really try to keep things in perspective, and I promise I'm not looking for signs or anything out of the ordinary. \n\nI hope that's the right answer? I just want to reassure you that I'm keeping a very level head about everything else. I'm not letting this work thing spill over into how I see the rest of the world. Does that sound okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any moments where you doubted your belief that these ensemble members are undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes, because I know that's probably what a reasonable person would do. And I really, really try to be reasonable. I don't want to be difficult or cause problems for anyone. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest, and I know I need to be honest with you... no, I haven't really doubted it. Like I said, it's just this absolute certainty I have. It's been almost two years now, right after that commission, and it's always those same few people. It's the schedule changes, the seating choices, the little comments during rehearsal... it just feels so clear to me that they're quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nEven when they show me emails or notes that supposedly explain why they did something... I just can't shake the feeling. I know that probably sounds terrible, and I'm so sorry if it makes me seem stubborn. I'm really not trying to be. I just... I can't help but see it that way. Is it... is it bad that I feel so certain about it? I hope you're not disappointed in me for saying that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your certainty about being undermined is an incorrect interpretation of the situation?\n\nPatient: I... I want to say yes. I really do. I know that's what I'm supposed to say, right? That I should be open to being wrong. And I try so hard to be the kind of person who gives everyone the benefit of the doubt. I don't want you to think I'm just this paranoid, difficult person who can't take criticism or work well with others. \n\nBut... gosh, I'm sorry, I just... I can't. I'm here because for almost two years now, ever since that commission, I just haven't been able to shake this absolute certainty. It's just those few people in the ensemble, and it's the schedule changes, the seating choices, the little comments during rehearsals... they're just quietly trying to undermine my work. \n\nEven when they pull up those emails or notes that supposedly explain why they did something... it doesn't change how certain I feel about what's really happening. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm frustrating you. I just... I can't see it as an incorrect interpretation, as much as I wish I could. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not being too difficult by saying that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people outside of your ensemble are watching you or talking about you?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. I mean, I know how that sounds, and I would tell you if I did, I promise. But no, it's really just... just those few people at work. \n\nWhen I'm out at the grocery store or, you know, just walking around, I don't feel like anyone is paying attention to me at all. I'm just a normal person going about my day. I don't think strangers care about me or what I'm doing. \n\nI really try to keep this whole thing contained to just that specific situation with the ensemble. I'm not... I'm not paranoid about the whole world, you know? I hope that makes you feel a little better about my situation. I'm really trying to be as grounded as possible about everything else. Is that... is that a good sign, that it's just the one thing?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for you to change your mind about these ensemble members undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I... I really want to be the kind of person who can change their mind. I know that's what a healthy, reasonable person would do, and I swear I've tried. I don't want you to think I'm just being stubborn or rigid. I know I keep saying this, and I'm so sorry if it's getting repetitive, but... it's just been almost two years now. Ever since that commission. \n\nI just haven't been able to shake this... this absolute certainty about it. It's just those few people, and it's the schedule changes, the way they seat people, the little comments during rehearsals... it's all just them quietly undermining my work. Even when they show me the emails or the notes that supposedly explain everything, I just... I can't change my mind. The feeling is just too strong. \n\nI know that's probably not the answer you were hoping for, and I feel terrible admitting it. I really do want to be flexible. Is it... is it really awful that I can't seem to let this go? I hope you're not too frustrated with me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the ensemble members is something that might change over time?\n\nPatient: I... I know I'm probably sounding like a broken record today, and I really apologize for being so stuck on this. I'd love to sit here and tell you that, yes, maybe in a few months it'll all just blow over and I'll see it differently. That's what a good, professional collaborator would do, right? \n\nBut honestly... it’s been almost two years since that commission. Two whole years of this. And no matter how much time goes by, or how many times they pull up an old email or a note to supposedly explain away a sudden schedule change or a weird seating arrangement, I just haven't been able to shake the certainty that they are quietly undermining my work. Those little comments in rehearsals from those few people... they still happen, and that feeling just doesn't fade. \n\nI feel terrible being so unyielding about it, especially when you're trying so hard to help me look at other perspectives. Am I... am I failing at this? I really want to be making progress with you, I promise I do, I just don't know how to turn off what I'm seeing. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally trust other people outside of this situation with the ensemble?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. I really want to be clear about that. Outside of work, I'm a very trusting person. I have good friends, I get along with my neighbors... I don't go around second-guessing everyone I meet. \n\nI know it might seem like I'm just a suspicious person because of how I feel about those few people in the ensemble, but I promise that's not who I am generally. I trust my family, my friends... even just people I interact with day-to-day, like at the coffee shop or whatever. I don't think everyone is out to get me. \n\nI really try to be a positive, open person in my regular life. I hope that helps show that I'm trying to keep a good perspective on things overall. Does that... does that sound like a healthy way to handle it? I just want to make sure I'm not letting this work thing ruin my whole outlook on life.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anyone else shares your belief that these ensemble members are undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I... I don't think so, no. I haven't really talked to anyone else about it, to be honest. I didn't want to start any drama or make anyone uncomfortable, you know? I want to be easy to work with. I try to keep things professional and positive, so bringing up my... my concerns to the rest of the group just didn't seem like the right thing to do. \n\nI'm pretty sure everyone else just thinks things are running normally. And I really don't want to be the guy complaining behind people's backs. I'm trying to handle this as responsibly as I can on my own. Is it... is it better that I've kept it to myself? I didn't want to cause a scene or make you think I'm causing trouble at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the ensemble members could be considered a delusion by others?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess I can see how someone might use that word if they only heard part of the story, or if they didn't know the whole context. Delusion is such a strong word, though. It makes it sound like I'm completely out of touch with reality, and I really hope you don't think that about me. \n\nI know it sounds unusual, me being so focused on this one thing with the ensemble. But I'm just trying to make sense of what I'm seeing and hearing at work. I'm not... I'm not crazy, you know? I'm just trying to be a good composer and protect the integrity of the piece. \n\nI suppose if I tried to explain it to someone who wasn't there, they might think I'm overreacting or imagining things. That's part of why I haven't told anyone else. I don't want people to think I'm difficult or... or unstable. Do you think I'm coming across that way? I really want to be reasonable about all this. I'm just trying to navigate a really stressful professional situation as best as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any evidence that could change your mind about the ensemble members undermining your work?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know. That's a really good question. I try to be open-minded, I really do. But... I'm here because for almost two years now, I've just been unable to shake this... this absolute certainty about what's happening. \n\nSince the commission, it's just been this pattern with a few people in the ensemble. The sudden schedule changes, the weird seating choices, the little comments they make during rehearsals... it all feels so deliberate. And they'll show me emails or rehearsal notes that supposedly explain why they did this or that, like, \"Oh, it was just a logistical thing,\" or \"We had a conflict.\" But... it doesn't change what I know is happening. The emails just feel like cover-ups, you know? \n\nI really wish I could just look at a schedule change and say, \"Okay, that's fine,\" but my brain just won't let it go. It feels like they're quietly undermining my work, and no piece of paper they show me seems to fix that feeling. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I don't sound completely stubborn. I really want to be flexible and easy to work with... I just can't seem to turn off this feeling that they're working against me. Is it okay that I feel that way, even with the \"proof\" they try to show me?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I... I don't know if they could completely change. I'm sorry, I feel like I'm just repeating myself, and I really don't want to frustrate you or seem like I'm not trying. 

It's just... like I mentioned, it's been almost two years since the commission, and this feeling is just so ingrained now. I really want to be the kind of person who can just let things go and completely change my perspective. I try to be so accommodating as a director. But this certainty that a few of them are using those schedule changes and seating arrangements and little comments to undermine me... it's just always there. Even when they show me the emails and notes that are supposed to explain it all away, I just can't shake it. 

I hope you don't think I'm being intentionally stubborn. I really want to be a good patient and give you the right answers. Do you think it's possible for someone to be reasonable in every other way, but just have this one thing they can't let go of? I'm really trying my best to work through this with you.

It feels like they're quietly undermining my work, and no piece of paper they show me seems to fix that feeling. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I don't sound completely stubborn. I really want to be flexible and easy to work with... I just can't seem to turn off this feeling that they're working against me. Is it okay that I feel that way, even with the \"proof\" they try to show me?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it would be possible for new information to make you question your beliefs about the ensemble members?\n\nPatient: I guess that's sort of similar to what we were just talking about, right? I... I really want to say yes, because I pride myself on being reasonable. And no matter how much time goes by, or how many times they pull up an old email or a note to supposedly explain away a sudden schedule change or a weird seating arrangement, I just haven't been able to shake the certainty that they are quietly undermining my work. Those little comments in rehearsals from those few people... they still happen, and that feeling just doesn't fade. \n\nI feel terrible being so unyielding about it, especially when you're trying so hard to help me look at other perspectives. Am I... am I failing at this? I really want to be making progress with you, I promise I do, I just don't know how to turn off what I'm seeing. I hope you can understand where I'm coming from.\n\nDoctor: Do you generally trust other people outside of this situation with the ensemble?\n\nPatient: Oh, yes, absolutely. I really want to be clear about that. Outside of work, I'm a very trusting person. I have good friends, I get along with my neighbors... I don't go around second-guessing everyone I meet. \n\nI know it might seem like I'm just a suspicious person because of how I feel about those few people in the ensemble, but I promise that's not who I am generally. I trust my family, my friends... even just people I interact with day-to-day, like at the coffee shop or whatever. I don't think everyone is out to get me. \n\nI really try to be a positive, open person in my regular life. I hope that helps show that I'm trying to keep a good perspective on things overall. I didn't want to cause a scene or make you think I'm causing trouble at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your belief about the ensemble members could be considered a delusion by others?\n\nPatient: I mean... It makes it sound like I'm completely out of touch with reality, and I really hope you don't think that about me. \n\nI know it sounds unusual, me being so focused on this one thing with the ensemble. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I really wish I could say yes to that. I know we keep circling back to this, and I feel awful that I can't just give you the 'yes' you're probably looking for. I really want to be open-minded... I always try to be a collaborative director. 

But... proven entirely wrong? I just don't see how. Like I said, it's been almost two years since that commission, and the certainty is just so deep now. When I see those specific people altering the schedule, or picking seating choices that mess with the acoustics, or making those little comments... I just know they're quietly undermining my work. 

Even when they hand me physical proof, like those emails or venue notes that supposedly explain it all away... I look at it, and I smile, and I want to believe it. I really do. But it just doesn't shake that certainty. 

I'm sorry, I know that's probably not what you want to hear, and I hate feeling like I'm letting you down. I just hope you know I'm trying my absolute best to be transparent with you. Does that help at all, or am I just making this harder for you?
